AfD 躍進で動揺する「防火壁」 
今後各種政策へのAfDの影響が強まる可能性

○ ドイツで行われた州議会選挙では与党が勢力を後退させた一方、AfDが大きく躍進した。近年の
景気低迷や旧東西ドイツ間の差異が同党の支持拡大要因になっていると推測される 
○ 最大野党として勢いを増すAfDは政権の政策運営にも影響を及ぼし得る。また、同党の勢力拡大
が、主要政党が極右との連携を拒む「防火壁」の維持を難しくしていることも争点になっている 
○ AfDは今後も党勢拡大が予想され、将来的には国政でも「防火壁」の維持が困難となり政権入り
の可能性もある。同党の影響力拡大はドイツのみならず欧州全体の政治・経済に影響するだろう

１．党勢拡大が続くドイツのための選択肢 
ドイツでは、9月にザクセン＝アンハルト州、 メクレンブルク＝フォアポンメルン州、 ベルリンで州
議会選挙が実施された。メルツ首相率いるキリスト教民主同盟（CDU）は、メクレンブルク＝フォア
ポンメルン州で議席獲得に必要な得票率5％を下回り、 結党以来、 州レベルで初めて議席がゼロとなる
など、惨敗を喫した（図表1）。選挙結果を受けて、メルツ氏の辞任を求める声も上がるなど、従来か
ら低迷していた同氏への支持と求心力は、一段と低下している。一方で、大きく躍進したのが極右政
党であるドイツのための選択肢（AfD）1だ。特に、ザクセン＝アンハルト州でAfDが単独過半数に迫
図表 1 州・都市州議会の政党別議席占有率 図表2 連邦議会選挙と欧州議会選挙における

る議席を獲得したことは、これまでAfDとの連携を拒否してきた主要政党の政治戦略の持続可能性に
関する議論を巻き起こしている。 
このところAfDは、州議会選挙に加え、2024年の欧州議会選挙や昨年の連邦議会選挙でも躍進し、
国政で野党第一党となるなど党勢の拡大を続けている（図表2）。本稿では、AfDの支持拡大の背景を
分析したうえで、今後のドイツ並びに欧州政治・経済への影響を考察する。 
２．旧東西ドイツ間の差異や近年の景気低迷が AfD の支持拡大要因に 
AfDが支持される背景には、旧東西ドイツ間に未だ存在する差異や近年の景気低迷があるとみられ
る。 
ここでは、AfDがどのような層に支持されて勢力を拡大しているのかを確認すべく、中井（2020、

### Page 3

2021）および川畑（2024）を参考に、ロジスティック回帰による2つのモデルを用いてAfD支持者の属
性を分析した。結果は図表3の通りだ（使用データや推計方法、結果の解釈については補論を参照）。 
まず、モデル1では説明変数のうち、性別、年齢、学生、自国民主政治満足度、欧州統合支持、移民
が自国文化に与える影響がAfD支持と統計的に有意な関係を持つことが確認された。川 畑（2024）は 、
モデル1で統計的に有意な結果が得られた変数のうち、自国民主政治満足度、 欧州統合支持、 移民が自
国文化に与える影響がAfD支持率に影響を及ぼす背景について、旧東西ドイツ間の差異が存在する可
能性を指摘している。図表2・4の通り、AfDは旧東ドイツに属していた新連邦州2で特に支持される傾
向にあることで知られる3。そこで、こうした地域性を踏まえて、モデル1で有意な結果が得られた変
数のうち旧東ドイツ地域の十分なサンプル数を確保できるものに旧東ドイツ地域の回答者を1 とする
ダミー変数を乗じた交差項を追加したモデル2を推計した。その結果、自国民主政治満足度、EU統合
支持、移民が自国文化に与える影響について有意な逆相関関係が確認された。これらの変数は、数値
が上昇するほど肯定的な回答である。つまり、特に旧東ドイツ州で自国民主政治への不満や欧州統合
への否定的態度、移民が自国文化に悪影響を及ぼすという懸念がAfD支持と強く結びついていると解
釈できる。 
ドイツは統一から35年が経過してもなお旧東西間の格差が残存している。例えば、一人当たりGDP
の水準は旧東ドイツ州の方が大幅に低いほか、若年層を中心に経済的に豊かな西部への流出が続いて
いることで、 西ドイツ地域と比較して人口減少が急速に進んでいる （図表5）。こうした構造的な問題
に加えて、ここ数年の経済状況も東部におけるAfDの支持拡大を後押ししているとみられる。東西の
実質GDPの推移を比較すると、ウクライナ侵攻後、旧東ドイツ地域の方が落ち込みが大きく、両地域
の差が拡大している（図表6） 。 エネルギーコスト高や対中競争激化等による製造業の低迷に加えて旧
西ドイツ地域対比でウェイトが大きい建設業が高金利や資材価格上昇により低迷していることが背景
とみられる。 
加えて、東西統一が経済や政治面で安定・発展していた旧西ドイツに旧東ドイツが編入される形で
図表 4 州別の AfD 支持率 図表5 ドイツの地域別の一人当たり GDP と人口

### Page 4

4

行われたことも、旧東ドイツ地域でのAfDの支持拡大につながったとみられる。旧西ドイツ主導の国
家体制整備が行われる過程で、東ドイツ地域の様々な分野の要職に西ドイツのエリート層が流入する
「エリート移転」が生じた。これが、東部出身者が経験や人的ネットワーク、昇進機会を得る余地を
狭め、 その後の持続的なキャリアアップの阻害要因になったと言われている4。実際、1990～2020年の
パネルデータを用いて東部出身者の管理職・上級管理職への到達機会を分析したHartmann（2024）に
よれば、東部地域の上級管理職において東部出身者は西部出身者より不利な地位に置かれているとし
ている。この差は学歴、職業経験、産業構造、社会的出身、失業経験等では十分に説明できず、統一
後の「エリート移転」の影響が持続している可能性を指摘している。また、Vogel et al.（2026）は、旧
東ドイツ地域出身者はドイツ人口の約19％を占めるにもかかわらず、同国の様々な分野の指導的ポス
トに占める東部出身者の割合は2024年時点で12.1％にとどまるとしており、依然として顕著な過少代
表が続いていることがうかがえる。 
こうした状況が自国民主政治への不満や政治・経済エリートへの反発につながり、「腐敗したエリ
ート対善良な市民」の構図で既存政府を批判するポピュリズムが拡大する要因になっていると考えら
れる。 現に、infratest dimapがザクセン＝アンハルト州で選挙当日に行った調査では、7割強の回答者が
「政治経済が旧西ドイツの人々に強く支配されている」、「旧東ドイツ地域住民は今も二等市民扱い
されている」との認識を示している。さらに、同調査では、旧東ドイツ地域の利益を最も代表できる
政党としてAfDを挙げた回答者の割合が最も多かった（図表7）。こうした結果は、東部住民の間に残
る代表性への不満や既成政治への反発が、AfDへの支持につながる土壌の一つになっていることを示
唆している。 
このような東部出身者の過少代表や二等市民意識は、主に東西統合を主導した旧西ドイツ出身の政
治エリートによって進められてきたEU統合等の政策への不信や不満を強めていると推測される。AfD
はこうした既成政治を批判しており、その訴えが旧東ドイツ地域での支持拡大を促す一因となってい
図表 6 ドイツ：地域別の実質 GDP 図表 7 旧東ドイツ地域の利益を

ると考えられる。 
また、旧東ドイツ地域は歴史的背景から移民が少なく文化的均質性が高いと言われている。このよ
うな社会構造が移民による自国文化への影響に対する懸念と移民受け入れに否定的なAfDの支持に強
く結びついていると考えられる5。  
３．今後、各種政策への AfD の影響が強まる可能性 
以上の複合的な要因からAfDは支持を拡大しているが、こうした傾向は今後も続くと予想される。 
旧東西ドイツ間の政治・経済的格差は短期的に解消可能なものではない。また、財政拡張によるイ
ンフラや防衛向けの投資増加を背景に景気は緩やかに持ち直しているものの、エネルギー価格の高止
まりや中国との競争激化等を背景に、これまでドイツ経済を支えてきた自動車や化学については回復
が見通せない。 加えて、 中東情勢緊迫化や猛暑の影響6によりインフレが押し上げられている。 Federle 
et al. （2024）は、予想外の物価上振れや景気下振れは急進的な政党の得票率を有意に押し上げると分
析しており、足元の物価上昇はAfDの追い風になり得る（図表8）。これらを踏まえれば、有権者の不
満は残存し、AfDが支持を得やすい状況は続くとみられる。 
また、AfDは旧東ドイツ地域を地盤としながら、特に男性を中心に若年・中年層では居住地域に関
わらず支持を広げている。 時系列データを用いているわけではないが、 前述のモデル2において年齢と
旧東ドイツダミーの交差項が有意な結果とならなかったことは、こうした状況を示唆している。同党
はSNSを通じた情報発信により、極右という印象を薄め、親しみやすい政党として浸透しているとみ
られる。今後、世代交代が進むことでAfDの支持は旧西ドイツ地域も含めて全国的に拡大することも
想定される。 
AfDは現時点では政権に参加していないものの、支持拡大により最大野党として無視できない勢力
となっている。 こうした状況を受けて、 与党はAfD
への支持者流出を抑えるため、 その政策や主張を
一定程度取り込む必要に迫られるだろう。AfDは、
減税や規制緩和・行政手続き簡素化、 エネルギー
コスト削減等に積極的であり、 これらの実現に向
けて政権に要求を強めることが想定される。 こう
した政策は、 家計の可処分所得や企業の事業環境
を改善するほか、 エネルギー集約型産業のコスト
負担を軽減することで景気にはプラスの影響を
与えるだろう。 
一方で、AfDが移民問題を争点化し支持を拡大
していることを踏まえれば、 移民政策はより厳格
化され得る。 実際、ショルツ前政権やメルツ政権
はAfDの支持拡大に押される形で、既に国境管理
や移民抑制策を導入している。AfDの勢力拡大は、
こうした政策の強化を一層促す要因となるだろ
う。 移民受入れの厳格化は人手不足を加速させる

### Page 6

6

ほか、外国人材や海外企業にとってのドイツでの就労や投資先としての魅力低下につながることもあ
る。また、AfDは、メルツ政権が年金財政の安定化を目的として検討している支給開始年齢引き上げ
を含む年金改革や、労働時間規制の緩和に反対している。今後、国民の負担増加につながり得るこう
した政策の実現は一層難しくなるだろう。 
EUの政策にも影響が出る可能性がある。 ドイツは、 欧州委員会が提示した2028～34年の次期EU中期
予算（MFF）案について、既に数千億ユーロ規模の削減を求め、EUの追加的な共同借入にも反対して
いる。EUに懐疑的なAfDの台頭は、 ドイツのEU予算の削減を求める立場を強め、 次期MFFの合意形成
を一段と難しくする恐れがある。 
AfDは、 民族的・文化的・宗教的属性に基づく国民観、 党関係者によるナチスを巡る歴史認識に関す
る言動などが問題視されており、ザクセン＝アンハルト州の憲法擁護庁は同州のAfD支部を「確定的
な極右」 と分類している7。 ナチ党の政権獲得とその後の独裁化という歴史的経験を踏まえて、 主要政
党はこうした極右政党の政権参加や法案成立過程での連携を拒否する「防火壁（Brandmauer）」と呼
ばれる方針を維持してきた。しかしながら、今回の州議会選挙では、一部地域で「防火壁」を維持し
AfDを除く政権を組成することが難しくなっている現実を突きつける結果となった。 AfDの支持拡大
が今後も予想されることを踏まえれば、こうした展開は将来的に国政においても十分に想定される。 
今回の州議会選挙8のようにAfDが連邦議会でも過半数に迫る議席を得た場合、 主要政党が 「防火壁」
を維持するには、党派性の異なる複数政党による連立が必要となる。このような状況では連立交渉の
難航・長期化は避けられず、その間政治・政策運営は停滞することになろう。また、政権樹立に成功
しても、連立内で方針がまとまらず政権基盤が不安定化することも考えられる。こうした政治の停滞
や不安定化は政策不確実性を高め、企業の設備投資などを抑制する要因となり得る。 
また、そもそも 「防火壁」を維持しない、 あるいは （AfDが過半数議席を獲得することで） 維持でき
なくなり同党が政権に参加するケースも将来的には想定される9。AfDが政策に直接的に関与すること
になれば、野党として他党の政策修正を促す場合と比べて前述の規制緩和や減税、エネルギー政策見
直し等が加速することで、低迷が続く一部製造業には追い風となるだろう。一方、財政や移民政策に
ついては懸念事項が残る。同党は財政規律を重視する姿勢を示しているものの、その公約は財源の裏
付けが乏しいと言われている10。財政への信認が損なわれることによる金利上昇や財源確保を目的と
する重要政策の予算削減とこれに伴う不確実性上昇のリスクが高まるだろう。移民の流入が大幅に減
少することで人手不足に拍車がかかり、インフレや景気下振れが深刻化するリスクも指摘されている
11。 
AfDはドイツのユーロ圏離脱とマルクの再導入や EUを主権国家間の緩やかな協力枠組みに再編す
ることを主張するなど、 国家主権重視の立場からEUに極めて懐疑的だ。 こうしたスタンスを踏まえれ
ば、 同党が政権に関与する場合、EUの重要政策目標の一つである市場の統合やEUレベルで進む防衛力
強化等の停滞が懸念される12。加えて、親ロシア的な立場をとり、 ロシアとの接触がたびたび問題にな
ってきたAfDが政権に加われば、対ロシア制裁やウクライナ支援に関するEU内合意形成が難しくなる
ほか、安全保障上の懸念を抱く他の欧州諸国やEUとの外交関係悪化も予想される。 
第二次世界大戦後、極右勢力の政権参加を抑制する政治的・制度的枠組みを維持してきたが、AfD
の勢力拡大はこうした枠組みに強い圧力をかけており、ドイツ政治は大きな曲がり角を迎えている。
AfDの支持拡大は、 ドイツのみならず欧州全体の政治・経済に影響を及ぼし得る。 それだけに、 州議会

### Page 7

7

選挙後の政局がどのように展開するのか、AfDがメルツ政権下の連邦政治でどこまで影響力を拡大す
るのか、そして与党をはじめとする主要政党が「防火壁」を維持しつつ有権者の不満にいかに応える
のかは、重要な焦点となろう。

補論 
（１） 推計に用いたデータ 
政治・社会・経済に関する態度および個人の属性について国際比較可能な個票データを提供する欧
州社会調査 （ESS）が 、2023年から2024年にかけて実施した調査のデータを使用した。なお、今回の分
析は特定の支持政党を持つ回答者のデータのみを用いている。 
（２） 推計手法と結果の解釈 
被説明変数にAfDを支持する者を1それ以外を0とする二値変数を、説明変数には回答者の基本的な
社会経済属性を規定する年齢 （実数） 、 性別 （男性＝1）、所 得（1～10の値をとり、10が最も高所得） 、
教育水準 （1～8の値をとり、8が最も高学歴） 、 職業状況 （就業者、 失業者、 主夫・主婦、 年金生活者、
障がい・疾病のそれぞれを1とする二値変数）に加えて、自己生活満足度（0～10の値をとり、10が最
も満足度が高い）、自国経済満足度（0～10の値をとり、10が最も満足度が高い）、自国民主政治満足
度（0～10の値をとり、10が最も満足度が高い）、欧州統合支持（0～10の値をとり、10が最も統合を
支持）、伝統重視（1～6の値をとり、6が最も伝統重視）、規範重視（1～6の値をとり、6が最も規範
重視）、愛国心（0～10の値をとり、10が最も愛国心が強い）、自由重視（1～6の値をとり、6が最も
自由重視）、移民が自国経済に与える影響（0～10の値をとり、10が最も肯定的）、移民が自国文化に
与える影響 （0～10の値をとり、10が最も肯定的） などの回答者の政治志向や移民への態度を表す変数
を採用し、ロジスティック回帰を行った。また、モデル2には旧東ドイツ地域に居住する回答者を1と
するダミー変数を追加している。なお、二値変数以外の説明変数の尺度は一様ではなく、係数の大小
比較に適さないため、標準化したうえで推計を行っている。 
図表3にまとめた結果の解釈については次の通りだ。モデル1の自国民主政治満足度を例にとると、
同変数が1標準偏差上昇するとAfDを支持するオッズ （＝AfD支持の起こりやすさ）は26％に低下する。
すなわち、自国の民主政治に対する満足度が高い人ほどAfDを支持しにくく、逆に民主政治への不満
が強い人ほどAfDを支持する傾向があることを示している。また、二値変数については、例えばモデ
ル1の性別であれば男性のAfDを支持するオッズは女性の235％（2.35倍）となることを意味する。

1 もともと AfD は、欧州債務危機の際にメルケル政権による南欧諸国への救済措置に不満を抱く経済学者らを中心に結成され、
「教授政党」とも呼ばれていた。当初は EU やユーロ圏からの離脱など、主に経済・通貨政策を掲げており、必ずしも現在のよ
うな排外主義的な政党ではなかったと言われている。しかし、その後は党内で右派的勢力が強くなったことに加え、シリアなど
から多くの難民が流入した 2015 年の欧州難民危機と、当時のメルケル政権による移民・難民政策への世論の反発を背景に排外
主義的な主張を前面に押し出す政党へと変質していった 
2 メクレンブルク＝フォアポンメルン州、ブランデンブルク州、ザクセン＝アンハルト州、ザクセン州、テューリンゲン州 
3 現在、AfD が州議会第一党となっているテューリンゲン州、ザクセン＝アンハルト州、メクレンブルク＝フォアポンメルン州
はいずれも新連邦州だ 
4 田中（2019） 
5 Arzheimer（2017）は、旧東ドイツ地域では戦後から東西統一前までドイツ社会主義統一党（SED）が支配的地位を占め、政党
間競争や自由選挙を経験してこなかったため、西ドイツのような特定政党への長期的な帰属意識が形成されにくかったと指摘し
ている。そのため、統一後に CDU や SPD などの西ドイツの政党が活動を拡大しても強固で安定した支持基盤を築くことは容易
ではなく、AfD のような新興政党が支持を獲得しやすい条件の一つになったとみられる 
6 猛暑が欧州の経済・物価に与える影響については川畑・北川（2026）を参照 
7 また、全国的にも、連邦憲法擁護庁は 2025 年に AfD 全体を「確定的な極右」と位置付けた（現在は司法判断が出るまで保留
されている） 
8 ドイツでは、州政府の構成員が連邦参議院（上院）において各州を代表する仕組みとなっている。各州には人口に応じて 3～6
票が配分され、法案等の審議・議決を通じて州の利害を連邦政治に反映する。今回の州議会選挙後に AfD が州政府に参画すれ
ば、連邦参議院における審議や投票を通じて連邦政治にも直接的に影響を及ぼすことになる。なお、各州の票は分割せず一括し
て投じなければならず、州政府を構成する政党間で投票方針を統一できない場合、当該州は事実上棄権となる 
9 「防火壁」を維持することで、AfD に「主要政党が民意を軽視している」という批判材料を与え、かえって同党を勢いづかせ
ているとも言われている 
10 Dorsch et al.（2026）は、AfD がザクセン＝アンハルト州議会選挙で打ち出していた公約を実現する場合、少なくとも年間で
22 億ユーロ（同州の税収の約 4 分の 1 に相当）の財源が不足すると試算している 
11 ザクセン＝アンハルト州を分析対象とした Gropp and Reifschneider（2026）は、AfD 主導の州政権成立に伴って排外的な政治
風土が強まり、移民の流入が減少すれば、2027～31 年の 5 年間で約 1 万 4,000 人の追加的な人手不足が生じ、18～32 億ユーロ
（同州 GDP の約 2～4％）の付加価値が失われると試算している。なお、Dorsch et al.（2026）についても言えることだが、州支
部と連邦レベルではスタンスの相違点も多いため単純化はできない点には留意が必要だ 
12 自国の主権強化や治安維持等の目的での防衛費増額には賛成している

堅調なAI関連需要を受け、製造 
業の景況感が改善すると予想

○ 業況判断ＤＩ予測：大企業・製造業   ＋25％Pt（6月調査から3ポイント改善） 
                  大企業・非製造業 ＋37％Pt（6月調査から横ばい） 
○ 2026年度設備投資計画(全規模・全産業)予測：前年度比＋7.8％（6月調査：同＋6.8％）

大企業・製造業の業況判断は
改善、大企業・非製造業の業
況判断は横ばいを予想 
10月1日公表の日銀短観（9月調査）では、大企業・製造業の業況判断DI
が＋25％Ptと、6月調査（＋22％Pt）から3ポイントの改善を予想（図表1）
する。関連指標では、7～9月期法人企業景気予測調査において、大企業の
「貴社の景況判断BSI」は製造業を中心に改善したほか、9月ロイター短観で
は、半導体やデータセンター（DC）関連の受注が好調との声が目立った。AI
関連需要が旺盛ななか、生産用機械や化学、非鉄金属などの関連業種で幅広
く景況感が改善すると見込む。

### Page 2

2

実際、AI関連財輸出の増勢は拡大している（図表2） 。半導体・電子部品の
輸出は、メモリ価格上昇の恩恵を受けて前年比で大幅プラスとなっているほ
か、足元では半導体製造・検査機器の輸出数量が急増している。その他、DC
に使われる冷却装置などの設備や、半導体製造に不可欠な原材料・素材の輸
出も増加するなど、輸出品目の裾野は広がっているようだ。こうしたもと、
AI関連財の生産指数は2025年9月比＋11.3％と大幅に上昇（図表3）してお
り、AI関連財の増産が鉱工業生産全体のけん引役になっている。 
また、足元にかけて円安基調が継続していることも、こうした輸出志向の
強い製造業種の景況感を下支えしている。 
大企業・非製造業の業況判断DIは＋37％Pt（6月調査：＋37％Pt）と、高
水準が継続する見込みだ。 
既往の資源高を背景に、卸売業の景況感が堅調となることが期待されるほ
か、堅調な投資需要を背景に、建設業でも良好な景況感が続きそうだ。さら
に、不動産価格や賃料の上昇を受けて、不動産業も好調を維持するとみてい
る。 
宿泊・飲食サービス等のインバウンド関連業種の景況感も底堅い結果が予
想される。訪日外客数については、短期的にはやや増勢が鈍化している。中
東情勢悪化を背景とした航空便の減便や、燃油サーチャージの上昇を受けた
航空運賃の負担増などが背景にある。日中対立を受けて中国の訪日外客数が
大幅減となっている状況も不変だ。もっとも、韓国や台湾など近隣諸国から
の訪日外客数は大幅な増加が続いており、全体としてみれば、大幅な腰折れ
は回避されている。

### Page 3

3

なお、製造業・非製造業に共通する要因として、中東情勢悪化を受けた石
化製品価格など原材料価格の上昇や人件費上昇といったコスト増による業績
下押し圧力は、日本企業全体としてみれば限定的なものに留まるとみてい
る。大企業やサプライチェーンの上流企業を中心に価格転嫁が進んでおり、
コスト増を一定程度吸収できている。上述の法人企業景気予測調査における
2026年度の経常利益計画が製造業・非製造業ともに上方修正され、増益見込
みに転じていることも、全体としての企業景況感が底堅さを維持するとみて
いる根拠の一つである。ただし、中小企業や、消費者に近いサプライチェー
ンの下流企業では十分な価格転嫁が進まず、景況感の下押し圧力となること
が予想される。 
先行きの業況判断DIは、製造業・非製造業ともに企業規模を問わず悪化が
見込まれる。もっとも、先行きの業況判断DIは現在対比で下振れしやすいク
セのある統計である。中東情勢が引き続き予断を許さないことなど、懸念材
料はあるが、実態としては、日本企業は先行きの景気をさほど悲観的に見て
いない可能性が高い。 
2026年度も、引き続き旺盛な
投資計画が示される見通し 
2026年度の設備投資計画 （全規模 ・ 全産業） は同＋7.8％（6月調査：＋6.8％）
と上方修正を予測する。従来からの旺盛な投資需要に加え、政府が7月以降開
始した「大胆な投資促進税制」に基づく投資支援策も、民間企業の投資需要
を下支えする公算が大きい。中東情勢悪化に伴う不確実性が前回調査時点か
ら幾分後退しているとみられることも、ポジティブな材料だ。 
日本政策投資銀行が公表した 「全国設備投資計画調査（2026年6月）」に よ
れば、化学・電気機械・非鉄金属・電力など、幅広い業種で旺盛なAI関連需
要に対応することを目的とした投資が計画されており、投資のけん引役とな
ることが期待される。 
不動産やサービス業では、ホテル開発や娯楽施設の高付加価値化といった
インバウンド関連投資も好調のようだ。自動車では、EV関連投資が一部減退
し、2025年度計画比では控えめな計画になることが予想されるものの、全体
としてみれば旺盛な投資需要が示されるだろう。

### Page 1

M
Update
Yageo Corp.  | Asia Pacific
TDK-Taiyo Yuden MOU: high-
end wall rises; cycle thesis
intact
The MOU validates AI-driven high-cap MLCC tightness, which
supports pricing and our MLCC up-cycle view.
TDK and Taiyo Yuden (both covered by Shoji Sato) signed an MOU to explore an
alliance in advanced MLCCs/inductors –  including manufacturing/procurement
cooperation, potential METI subsidies under Japan's Economic Security Act, and a
possible future capital tie-up.

Our take: We estimate MLCC demand next year at more than 40bn units of 47uF+,
which crowds out  midrange/low-end MLCC capacity.
1. We believe  de-specing to ≤22uF MLCCs is not a viable workaround.
Substituting high-cap MLCCs with larger counts of lower-capacitance parts
requires broad real estate that simply does not exist on today's densely
packed GPU/accelerator boards.
2. Switching to tantalum-polymer capacitors merely trades one shortage for
another, given equally tight tantalum supply.

3. So the only realistic industry response, in our view, is reallocating midrange/
low-end MLCC capacity toward high-cap production. That would structurally
tightens midrange/low-end supply, underpinning broad-based pricing power.
Things to watch:
• Whether a capital alliance materializes,
• METI subsidy size (this determines how distorted the high-end cost curve
gets),
• Counter-move by Murata (also covered by Shoji Sato) counter-move, and
• Any sign the alliance extends into mid-cap products.

See our latest report on MLCC here: Global Technology: MLCC: Continued Demand
Growth for High-Value-added Products for AI & Data Centers (15 Sep 2026)

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
미국 록빌·5공장 가세로 3분기 기대치 부합 전망 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 연결 매출액은 1조 3,637억 원(+8.4% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,092억 원(-3.8% YoY, 영업이익률 44.7%)으로 시장 기대치에 부합할 
전망이다. 5월 파업에 따른 생산 차질에도 1~4공장(총 60.4만 리터)의 풀가동과 
신규 공장의 매출 기여가 외형 성장을 뒷받침할 것으로 예상한다. 미국 록빌 
공장(6만 리터)은 3분기부터 매출 기여가 본격화되고, 5공장(18만 리터)도 
인증용(PPQ) 배치 생산을 통해 매출이 확대될 전망이다. 2분기에는 미국 공장·5공장 
관련 비용이 선반영됐으나, 3분기에는 매출 증가에 따른 고정비 부담 완화로 
영업이익률이 전분기 대비 0.3%p 상승할 것으로 예상한다. 
항체 중심 CDMO에서 펩타이드로 확장 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 신규 수주는 약 1.2조 원으로 전년 수주액의 약 20% 
수준에 그치고 있으나, 연말부터 내년까지 수주 모멘텀은 점차 강화될 것으로 
판단한다. 2025년 하반기 이후 미국의 의약품 관세 불확실성이 완화되며 글로벌 
제약사와의 논의가 늘어나고 있어, 신규 수주 계약으로 이어질 것으로 기대된다. 
11월 유상증자 납입이 마무리되면 6공장(18만 리터) 착공과 스위스 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 본격화되며 중장기 성장 기대감도 높아질 전망이다. 특히 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 연내 완료되면 2027년부터 연결 실적에 반영되는 동시에, 항체 의약품 
중심의 CDMO 사업을 GLP-1 등 펩타이드로 확장하는 계기가 될 전망이다. 
목표주가 200만 원으로 하향, 수주 회복이 관건 
삼성바이오로직스의 목표주가를 209만 원에서 200만 원으로 하향한다. 유상증자 
신주 발행에 따른 주당가치 4.7% 희석 효과를 반영했다. 폴리펩타이드 인수 효과는 
거래완료 이후 실적 추정치에 반영할 예정이다. 노조 파업 불확실성이 주가에 
부담으로 작용할 수 있어 신규 수주 모멘텀의 회복이 필요한 시점이다.

Editorial 
A Fed credibility-induced bounce  
Strategy summary:  The Fed’s unanimous decision to hike 
rates this week for the first time since 2023 , and pencil in 
another hike by December, confirms our expectations – flagged 
in the last two weeklies - of a central bank willing to establish its 
independence against political pressure. The Fed’s moderately 
hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. 
The BoJ also hiked rates this week. It is the first time on record 
all three major central banks – Fed, ECB and BoJ – have hiked 
in the same month , as they respond primarily to a resurgence 
in energy-driven inflation amid tight job markets.  
We expect the Fed to hike once more by year-end to 4.0-4.25% 
and follow up with another hike in H1 2027 to 4.25 -4.5%. With 
the revised estimates, we revise up our US government bond 
yield forecasts. Nevertheless, with Fed policy uncertainty 
easing, we see an opportunity to use the equity market dip to 
add exposure amid solid corporate earnings.  
Fed moves to restore credibility:  The Fed’s policy pivot this 
week followed Chair Warsh’s hawkish Jackson Hole speech 
and recent economic data prints, namely: a pick -up in job 
creation and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in 
oil prices due to the re -escalation of the Middle E ast conflict. 
Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike rates this week, we 
expect two more hikes by  June 2027 on the back of  robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated. We expect this pivot to help the Fed 
rebuild credibility as an inflation fighter.  
Policy outlook less aggressive than market pricing : The 
updated Fed policy rate estimates are lower compared with 
money market pricing of three more rate hikes by June 2027 
since we expect disinflation to take hold by Q2 2027 as the 
impact of oil prices and tariffs fade. We believe policy rates can 
return to the 4.0–4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility 
of cuts in H2 2027 as inflation pressures ease.  
Upgrading near-term US rates and USD estimates : Based 
on these revised estimates, we also upgrade our 3-month target 
for the US 10 -year government bond yield to 5.0 -5.25%. We 
also revise up our 12 -month target for the US 10 -year bond 
yield to 4.75-5.0% (see page 4).  
Buying the equity dip as focus turns to fundamentals: Fed 
hikes typically do not hurt equities over the medium-term when 
accompanied by strong earnings growth. We estimate a 25bps 
rise in Fed rates (the discount rate) would impact the S&P500 
index and global equities by 3 -4%. However, we expect the 
headwind from higher rates to be offset by strong earnings 
growth, driving positive performance for global equities.  
Furthermore, a hawkish Fed is largely priced in and we expect 
the Fed to deliver fewer rate hikes than currently priced by 
markets. Meanwhile, we believe concerns over AI industry 
leaders slowing the pace of development are overdone. Hence, 
we maintain our preference for a diversified group of large cap 
technology and semiconductor industry leaders (see page 5-6). 
BoJ hikes; modestly bullish JPY : The BoJ hiked rates by 
25bps to a three-decade high of 1.25%, as expected, but the 7-
2 split vote led to renewed JPY weakness on concerns it may 
slow the pace of further hikes. We remain constructive on JPY 
amid narrowing rate differential vs peers as the BoJ potentially 
hikes rates by 25bps once per quarter until Q2 2027 to curb 
rising domestic wage-driven inflation pressure . Any sign of BoJ 
slowing the pace of hikes could lead to renewed JPY weakness, 
fuelling inflationary pressures (see page 7).  
BoE holds; rangebound GBP: The BoE held rates this week, 
bucking the hiking trend among peers, as weakening UK job 
and housing markets offset rebound in energy-driven inflation. 
We expect BoE to deliver fewer rate hikes than the 100bps of 
increase currently priced by markets  as the job market slows 
further. This is likely to keep GBP rangebound (see page 7).  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions 
  Where do you see US government bond  yields following 
the Fed’s 16 September decision to hike interest rates? 
Our view: The US 10-year government bond yield is likely to 
retest the 5.00-5.25% range in the near term. We favour 3-7-year 
maturity bond s in t he US, preferring credit over government 
bonds. We are looking for an opportunity to extend duration 
approaching Q2 2027. 
Rationale: Over the next few months, we expect 2- and 30-year US 
government bond yields to remain rangebound, but the 10-year yield 
is expected to rebound up to the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons for 
this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially rise further 
due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle East, with Saudi 
Arabia’s East-West oil pipeline shut, the Houthis now firmly in control 
of the Strait of Bab-al-Mandab, the Strait of Hormuz still closed and 
China returning as a buyer of crude oil in the market ; 2) despite 
notable AI leaders expressing the need to slow AI development, AI 
capex plans are showing no signs of slowing down, which continues 
to drive above-trend growth in the economy. This, in turn, also means 
AI-related heavy bond issuance  is expected to continue. These 
issuers typically issue long-dated tenors, and this competition for 
capital will also exert upward pressure on the US 10-year yields. 
We note that the US 10-year bond yield has room to move lower, 
driven by short-covering activities in the very near term as net-short 
positions had moved to extreme levels before th is week’s  Fed 
meeting. However, we think such downshifts would be short-lived 
and the central tendency for the 10-year yield would be to move back 
up to the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Meanwhile, we see US 30-year yields continuing to reflect the dire 
fiscal outlook for the US government. We expect US 2-year yields 
to continue trading in a range that reflects expectations of further 
federal funds rate hikes. For the record, we only expect two more 
rate hikes from the Fed into the middle of 2027 , while money 
markets are pricing in three more for the same period. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate (either 
through a resolution of the M iddle East conflict or a re-routing of oil 
supply chains)  and the inflationary effects from tariffs to fade. 
Inflation by then is likely be close to – if not at – the Fed’s target of 
2%. At this point, we see room for the Fed to contemplate rate 
cuts. US 2-year yields (the tenor that is most sensitive to Fed policy 
changes) will likely start to price th is in. With oil prices and inflation 
cooling, 10-year yields would also have room to come down. 
US 30-year yields, on the other hand, are expected to  remain 
elevated as the US fiscal deficit remains substantial next year and 
could become much worse than its already dire state today. As a 
result, the ‘term premium ’ may keep the ultra -long end elevated. 
Short of some unorthodox policy to artificially cap them lower, 30 -
year yields could continue to drift higher into H2 2027. 
We continue to favour the 3-7-year segment of the yield curve and 
prefer credit over government bond s. We see an opportunity for 
us to extend duration closer to Q2 2027. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO for Fixed Income and FX

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Will the step -up in the  Fed’s risk-free rate trigger a re -
pricing of equities? 
Our view: We expect strong corporate earnings growth to offset 
the headwind of higher rates , driving positive performance for 
global equities. We remain Overweight global equities. 
Rationale: The Fed’s latest 25bps rate hike had been anticipated 
by the bond market, partly reflected in the bond yield  rise we have 
seen so far in 2026. Using the US 10-year government bond yield as 
a proxy for the risk -free rate, the yield has risen by 80-85bps this 
year. We estimate that each 25bps rise in the discount rate presents 
a valuation drag on global equities. On this basis, the rise in yields 
represents a potential 10-14% headwind for equities this year. 
However, the Fed’s hike reflects a solid expansion of US economic 
activity, resilient domestic spending and robust capital investment. 
This is consistent with the positive earnings revisions we have seen 
this year, with  2026 earnings growth projections now at 34% (and 
16% for 2027). Global equities are up 14% year-to-date (YTD), but 
even with a 14% valuation headwind from higher yields, we believe 
they can rise further, given strong earnings growth in 2026 and 2027. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is the outlook for the US 10-year government bond 
yield? What is your tactical view on the DXY? 
Our view: We raise our three-month US 10 -year government 
bond yield forecast to the 5.00-5.25% range. Meanwhile, we see 
a near-term upward bias in the DXY after its recent downtrend, 
supported by recent Fed tightening and higher  short-term US 
bond yields.  
Rationale: The Fed 's decision  to raise the target range for the 
federal funds rate by 25bps to 3.75-4.00% validates the upside policy 
risks we flagged recently, following Fed Chair Warsh's Jackson Hole 
speech and a run of firmer US economic data. We expect growth 
to remain robust, the labour market  to remain tight and inflation to 
stay elevated, supporting one more hike before year-end 2026 and 
potentially another in H1 2027  – fewer than the three additional 
hikes currently priced in by money markets . For the US 10-year 
government bond yield, further policy tightening and lingering US 
fiscal concerns should keep yields elevated, but the Fed’s visible 
response to inflation should limit additional upward pressure, leaving 
the 10-year yield holding around the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Crucially, Warsh provided no forward guidance on further rate 
hikes. Markets had already priced in substantial additional rate hikes 
ahead of the Fed meeting, reducing the scope for further USD upside 
purely from rate repricing. Meanwhile, we expect further policy 
tightening from the ECB and the BoJ, while the RBA also remains 
biased towards further tightening. This should gradually narrow the 
US rate advantage and limit the extent of a DXY rebound. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Anthony Naab, CFA, Investment Strategist

Global equities’ earnings growth estimates have 
been revised higher this year, offsetting the 
headwind from higher rates

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in technology as AI lab 
CEOs call for a slowdown in frontier AI model development? 
Our view: We believe concerns over “pacing the frontier” are 
overdone. We maintain our preference to remain invested in 
and diversified across Big Tech and semiconductor leaders. 
Rationale: We believe it is prudent for investors to avoid 
overreacting to the recent calls for a slowdown in frontier AI model 
development, as they focus on responsible AI development, rather 
than halting AI model development or reducing spending. The global 
AI leadership  race, reinforced by the US and China’s  strategic 
ambitions, also makes a coordinated slowdown unlikely. 
While the near-term narrative may shift from AI training beneficiaries 
towards inference plays, hyperscalers continue to signal strong AI 
infrastructure commitments and improving monetisation, supporting 
our AI capex outlook. Rising AI adoption and agentic use cases 
should sustain inference demand, even if training activity moderates, 
benefiting custom AI chips, NAND flash memory and hyperscalers.  
We believe the decline in valuations  this year more than 
compensates for any downside risk to AI capex. Our Opportunistic 
idea on Global Semiconductors further reflects our conviction, with 
attractive valuations supporting selective additions during volatility. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  With ASEAN and UK equities outperforming AI-focused 
markets in H2 2026, is it worth adding to these markets? 
Our view: ASEAN and UK equities offer portfolio diversification, 
but we are Underweight both markets in favour of US and Asia 
ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, given their stronger earnings potential. 
Rationale: The ASEAN and UK markets have outperformed the 
MSCI ACWI over the past three months, but both have 
underperformed on a YTD basis. Financials is the largest sector in 
the ASEAN (53%) and the UK (28%) and provides steady earnings. 
However, both markets have minimal exposure to the technology 
sector (just 3% and 1% , respectively), ultimately dragging down 
growth. Hence, while defensive markets offer value by increasing 
diversification and reducing volatility in a portfolio, we maintain our 
Underweight allocation for ASEAN and the UK, as we see much 
stronger earnings potential elsewhere in the US and AxJ markets.  
We remain Overweight US and AxJ, supported by robust earnings 
growth, as  AI still has the potential to become a major economic 
growth driver . Our 12-month S&P500 Index forecast of 8,400  
implies low-teens upside, supported by a strong earnings backdrop 
of 25-30% earnings-per-share growth for 2026 and 15 -20% for 
2027. AxJ also boasts the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, fuelled by the AI semiconductor super-cycle. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is it still appropriate to have a positive stance on US 
high-yield (HY) bonds? 
Our view: We remain opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. 
Lower sensitivity to interest rate volatility, a below-trend default 
rate and a maturity wall pushed out to 2028-29 drive this view. 
Rationale: Recent interest rate volatility has highlighted a key 
structural advantage of US HY bonds: their higher coupon rates  
and shorter tenors relative to Developed Market (DM) government 
bonds and US investment-grade (IG) corporate bonds translate into 
a much lower sensitivity to interest rates, cushioning bond prices 
during yield backup episodes, where bond yields rise sharply. 
YTD, US HY has delivered 1.8% in total returns, outperforming DM 
government bonds and US IG corporate bonds by over 3% each on 
a relative basis. This outperformance was particularly evident during 
the recent back-up in bond yields, as inflation concerns re-emerged 
after renewed Middle East tensions – an environment where HY's 
lower rate sensitivity provided a relative cushion  even as broader 
fixed income markets came under pressure in absolute terms. On 
the fundamentals side, US HY corporate credit quality remains 
resilient. Default rates continue to track below long-run trend levels, 
and the maturity wall has been pushed out to 2028 -29, reducing 
near-term refinancing risk. Against a non -recessionary macro 
backdrop, we expect these credit fundamentals to hold, supporting 
the case for continued opportunistic exposure to the asset class.  
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Do the latest BoJ and BoE meetings alter your view on the 
central banks’ rate paths and their corresponding currencies? 
Our view: We remain constructive on the JPY and expect two 
BoJ rate hikes this year and potentially one hike per quarter in 
H1 2027 as policy normalisation continues. Meanwhile, the BoE 
held rates at 3.75%. We expect GBP/USD to stay rangebound 
within 1.32-1.36 over the next 1-3 months, with downside risk 
towards the lower end of our medium-term 1.30-1.36 range. 
Rationale: Persistent inflation, higher energy prices and earlier JPY 
weakness support further BoJ policy normalisation . Continued 
hiking should progressively narrow the US -Japan rate gap, reduce 
the attractiveness of JPY -funded carry trades and support  the JPY 
over the medium term towards the 153-155 range. However, with a 
September hike largely priced  in, future tightening ’s pace  will be 
more important for the JPY. Meanwhile, the BoE was split 6-3 in its 
decision to hold rates at 3.75% , with three members preferring a 
25bps hike, signalling elevated inflation risks. However, a weaker 
labour market is likely to limit the pace of BoE tightening. The US-
UK rate divergence is expected to favour the USD over the next 1-3 
months. Further BoE tightening (if inflation remains elevated ) could 
narrow the rate gap and provide support to the GBP later in the cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist

We expect US HY bonds to continue delivering 
strong performance in the near term

Document 138: AI光环褪色、外资加速撤离，韩股从全球最强变全球最弱

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI光环褪色、外资加速撤离，韩股从全球最强变全球最弱.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

曾是全球 AI 交易最热⻔标的的韩国股市，正经历急剧降温。 Kospi 上半年领涨全球，下半年
却已下跌 22% ，沦为全球最差主要股指。外资今年撤资 1310 亿美元，成交额较峰值暴跌
70% ，散⼾杠杆资⾦也⼤幅萎缩。三星电⼦季度利润暴增近九倍却遭股价下跌。多家机构
转⽽增配中国台湾股市。
摘要：
韩国股市曾是今年全球 AI 交易最炙⼿可热的标的，如今却⾯临买家荒。
今年上半年，韩国 Kospi 以全球主要股指中最强的表现吸引了全球资⾦涌⼊；进⼊下半年，同⼀
个市场已累计下跌 22% ，成为全球最差。与此同时，同样 AI 概念浓厚的中国台湾和美国股市却在
不断刷新历史⾼点。
据彭博报道，外资今年从韩股净撤资 1310 亿美元，为亚洲主要市场之最。市场成交额较 5 ⽉底峰
值暴跌 70% ，散⼾和机构同步撤退。
两家芯⽚巨头，⼀个过度集中的赌注
韩国 Kospi 的涨跌，⼏乎被三星电⼦和 SK Hynix 这两家存储芯⽚巨头所主导。两者合计占 Kospi
权重逾 50% ，是今年上半年指数飙升的核⼼驱动⼒，如今却成了拖累。

Rayliant Global Advisors 投资组合管理主管 Phillip Wool 直⾔： “ 对⼤多数投资者⽽⾔ —— 尤其是
那些最近才因存储芯⽚交易⼊场韩国的⼈ —— 这个主题⾥容易赚的钱已经赚完了。 ” 他表⽰，其基
⾦已在韩国 AI 股上获利了结，⽬前对 SK Hynix 和三星电⼦均持低配。
市场的怀疑情绪在上周四被直⽩地呈现出来：三星电⼦季度营业利润同⽐暴增近九倍，股价却
不涨反跌。
回购潮将尽，买盘⽀撑⾯临真空
⽀撑韩股的另⼀根柱⼦也在松动。
三星和 SK Hynix 合计约 55 万亿韩元（约 410 亿美元）的股票回购计划即将完成。据摩根⼤通数
据，上个⽉韩国市场约 230 亿美元的买单中，两家芯⽚公司的回购占了⼤头。
⼀旦回购结束，市场将⾯临明显的买盘真空。
Julius Baer ⾹港研究主管 Richard Tang 表⽰： “ 我们看到资本正越来越多地回流⾄美国股市，这
加剧了韩国市场的持续外资流出。 ”
散⼾退场，杠杆资⾦仍未恢复
散⼾曾是 Kospi 上半年累计翻倍⾏情的重要推⼿，但 7 ⽉的暴跌令他们⾄今未能归来。

据韩国⾦融投资协会数据，融资余额在过去⼀个⽉维持在约 33 万亿韩元，远低于 6 ⽉峰值的 38.6
万亿韩元。券商账⼾中等待⼊市的投资者存款也从近 140 万亿韩元的历史⾼位降⾄约 100 万亿韩
元。

### Page 1

Taiwan Industrials

Investment case: We initiate coverage of Acter, an established 
multinational cleanroom engineering, procurement, construction and 
management (EPCM) turnkey service provider, with a Buy (1) rating. Its 
core business includes cleanroom electromechanical engineering, 
water/gas/chemical delivery systems, and other cleanroom services. We 
believe now is a good time to revisit the stock as it underperformed the 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly on weaker-than-expected 2Q26 
results and soft sector sentiment. Given its stronger revenue momentum 
over recent months and upcoming TSMC results on 15 October, which may 
drive sentiment, we suggest investors to buy the stock. We forecast a 
24.6% revenue CAGR and 36.9% net profit CAGR over 2026-28E.

Intact global demand for cleanroom technology, the edge AI market, 
and rapid cleanroom expansion in Asia further support Acter’s order 
backlog. Its backlog reached TWD66bn in July 2026 vs. TWD46bn in 2025 
and TWD30bn in 2022 (15.3% 2022-25 CAGR), which largely outpaced the 
global cleanroom technology market’s forecast CAGR over 2023-2032E, 
according to Precedence Research.

Strong competitive edge supports order share gains across multiple 
segments. Acter’s competitive edge lies in 1) intelligent management 
enabled by digital twins and AI, using 3D building information modelling 
(BIM) and AI-driven digital twin management; 2) intellectualisation of green 
engineering, which comprises green design, green procurement, and green 
techniques; 3) experience across multiple industries (ie, data centres and 
electromechanical engineering for the consumer industry) which supports 
better integration; and 4) a complete network, enabling on-the-spot support. 
Acter believes it has the highest market share among the operators of three 
major US data centre players due largely to its capabilities in liquid cooling 
and its expertise in electromechanical and cleanroom engineering.

Catalysts: Near-term: project wins from key clients, supporting stronger 
monthly revenue momentum; mid- to long-term: an improving operating-
margin trend and a resilient order backlog.

Valuation: We are the first non-domestic broker to cover Acter. We initiate 
coverage with a 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based on a target PER of 20x 
(equivalent to a 0.54x PEG over 2026-28E). We believe our valuation is 
fair, given Acter’s strong earnings visibility, which we believe warrants a 
rerating. Our TP is supported by a DCF valuation of TWD1,454.

Risks: 1) weaker-than-expected expansion in the semiconductor industry; 
and 2) project delays or cancellations.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026

How do we justify our view? 
Growth outlook Valuation Earnings revisions

Growth outlook

Acter: net profit and net-profit growth    
We forecast Acter to record a 36.9% net profit CAGR over 
2026-28E, on the back of a 24.6% revenue CAGR over the 
same period, driven by 1) larger project scale, as strong 
demand for cleanroom EPCM services from the high-tech 
sector is likely to support continued growth in Acter’s order 
backlog and 2) improving gross margins and operating 
efficiency. We expect operating margin to expand by 2.0pp 
in 2027E and 1.3pp in 2028E, driven by favourable gross 
margins on new projects and operating leverage.

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Valuation   Acter: 1-year-forward PER bands    
Our 12-month TP of TWD1,415 is based on a target PER 
of 20x, applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Our 
target multiple of 20x is higher than the stock’s past-3-year 
1-year-forward PER (12.2x) and past-5-year 1-year-
forward PER (9.6x) and implies a PEG of 0.54x on our 
36.9% net profit CAGR forecast over 2026-28E. We 
believe the stock deserves a rerating in the next 12 
months, largely supported by resilient cleanroom demand 
globally.

Our TP is also supported by an alternative DCF-derived 
valuation of TWD1,454/share.

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa forecasts

Earnings revisions

Acter: Bloomberg consensus EPS forecast revisions   
Bloomberg consensus EPS estimates for 2026E and 
2027E have risen 24.8% and 24.3% YTD, respectively. 
This is likely due to upward revisions to order backlog and 
earnings forecasts following better-than-expected industry 
outlook. We are the first foreign broker to cover Acter.

Source: Bloomberg

Company profile 
Established in 1979, Acter Group (Acter) is a leading engineering services provider specialising in 
cleanroom, electromechanical, and high-tech facility integration solutions for semiconductor, 
optoelectronics, biotech, green energy, and advanced manufacturing clients across Taiwan, China and 
the rest of the world. As of 2025, its revenue comprised cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas 
and chemical delivery systems (20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and 
high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

### Page 5

5

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Acting on the demand for cleanrooms 
Why are cleanrooms a bottleneck in the high-tech 
industry? 
In recent years, cleanrooms have become a critical bottleneck for high-tech industries, 
especially semiconductor manufacturing, OSAT, AI hardware, and PCB, mainly because of 
its indispensable nature, severe supply-demand imbalance, labour shortages, and high 
technical and regulatory barriers.

Cleanrooms are an indispensable core infrastructure as they determine product 
yield and quality stability. Advanced manufacturing nodes in semiconductors, 
optoelectronics, and biopharmaceuticals require highly particle-free, constant-temperature, 
and constant-humidity environments to ensure reliable production. As process 
technologies continue to advance, such as 2nm node (or even newer nodes) and 
advanced packaging, existing cleanrooms must be rebuilt, and the complexity of building 
new ones thus increases. This keeps overall demand for cleanrooms at high levels.

A severe global supply-demand imbalance with demand for cleanroom construction 
outpacing supply. The surge in AI servers, high-performance computing (HPC), and high-
bandwidth memory (HBM) has triggered a global expansion for front-end wafer fabs, 
packaging and testing facilities, and PCB manufacturing. Order visibility remains extremely 
strong across the industry. According to Markets and Markets, the global cleanroom market 
size is set to expand at a CAGR of 6.6% over 2025-31E. At the same time, the global 
semiconductor cleanroom market size is set to grow at a CAGR of 8.0% over 2025-30E. 
For instance, Acter’s order backlog exceeded TWD66bn at the end of July 2026, up from 
TWD46bn at the end of 2025, showing the urgency of demand for cleanroom construction.

Global: cleanroom market size and growth      Global: semiconductor cleanroom market size and growth

Source: Markets and Markets  Source: Markets and Markets

Human resources are an additional limitation to capacity growth. Cleanroom 
engineering is highly technical and labour-intensive, relying on specialised on-site 
installation and supervision that cannot be replaced by robots. At the same time, the 
industry faces severe labour shortages and an aging workforce, and younger generations 
are reluctant to join field operations. Cross-border staff deployment is also less cost-
efficient than local hiring.

Cleanrooms involve complex system integration, creating high barriers to entry. A 
cleanroom construction includes 4 sequential phases, where each phase usually requires 
totally different areas of expertise. Therefore, the competitive landscape in each area 
differs. The process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the physical 
plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for essential utility 
generation, where Acter is one of the leading global experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
network and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. As

### Page 6

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
global supply chains restructure under N+1 (domestic market + 1) strategies, companies 
building overseas facilities in places like the US face strict and complicated licensing 
processes, particularly on fire safety and hazardous chemicals. Without experience dealing 
with such engineering and safety issues, construction timelines can be severely delayed.

As a result, even with advanced equipment and chip designs, a high-tech company cannot 
begin production without adequate cleanroom space, skilled engineers, and system-
integration capabilities. Therefore, cleanroom engineering contractors hold significant 
bargaining power and capacity allocation leverage in today's tech supply chain.

IC foundry 
In the foundry sector, where the cleanroom supply-demand shortage is the tightest, project 
activity remains centred on advanced process capacity expansion in Taiwan and Arizona. 
Simultaneously, domestic advanced node development remains intense, with multi-phase 
fab builds advancing across Hsinchu, Taichung, and Kaohsiung. Local cleanroom and MEP 
integration demand is further boosted by advanced packaging facilities in Taiwan (such as 
Chiayi and Tainan) and upcoming projects in the US are set to contribute revenue for the 
cleanroom providers in the following years.

Memory 
The memory segment is exhibiting robust momentum as well, mainly catalysed by HBM 
capacity additions. Projects such as the plant in Japan, transitioned to contribution for the 
cleanroom EPCM providers starting from 2026. In Taiwan, turnkey engineering contracts in 
Miaoli are also actively underway. This trend is reinforced by stronger-than-expected 
expansion activity from memory producers in China.

OSAT,  PCB and ODM/OEM 
For OSAT companies, demand is also surging due to the spill-over effect of advanced 
packaging protocols and N+1 supply chain restructuring. The OSAT names are expanding 
their footprint both in Southern Taiwan and in overseas locations including in the US and 
Southeast Asia. Major engineering contracts signed between OSAT clients and Acter show 
stable long-term workload for strong cleanroom demand over our forecast horizon. 
Similarly, overseas expansion for PCB manufacturing is peaking in Southeast Asia, 
especially in Thailand. We expect Chinese PCB players to also increase their capacity, just 
like memory companies in the past year. Except for front-end and back-end chip 
manufacturing, ODM/OEM and data centre infrastructure is also a key growth engine for 
the EPCM industry. Major Taiwanese ODMs are building production facilities in Texas and 
Mexico to meet local manufacturing needs. Cleanroom contractors, such as Acter, 
providing MEP and system integration in Texas are directly benefiting from these projects. 
The shift toward liquid cooling systems has also increased engineering complexity, 
favouring few cleanroom companies with the expertise to deliver complex integration for 
CSPs in both North America and Southeast Asia.

Acter: an integrated cleanroom solutions provider  
Acter Group (Acter), an integrated cleanroom solutions provider, maintains its competitive 
positioning within the high-tech cleanroom and MEP engineering sector, driven by its group 
synergy and global footprint. Acter operates as a comprehensive vendor through a group 
business model. The parent company, Acter, supervises overall cleanroom and MEP 
services, while subsidiary Nova Technology (Novatech, 6613 TT, not rated) mainly provides 
chemical primary system and hook-up, and Novatech’s subsidiary Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated) provides gas primary system, hook-up and equipment. In China, Acter operates 
through Acter Technology Integration Group (603163 CH, not rated) and Suzhou Winmax 
(not listed) for Chinese EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management) 
and equipment business. Except for C&S (civil and structural) works, where the margin is 
low (7-8%) and the contract duration is long, Acter’s services span nearly the entire 
cleanroom construction process. Its one-stop solutions model supports customer retention 
Acter provides

solutions via various 
subsidiaries

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
and improves bid-win rates, particularly for overseas expansion, where customers have 
few reliable providers to choose from.

Acter: organisational chart

Source: Company, Daiwa

To avoid the risk of over-concentration, Acter diversifies its client portfolio across 
semiconductor foundries, memory, PCB, OSAT, ODM hardware assembly, CSP data 
centres and the consumer industry. By sector, semiconductor projects represent the largest 
share of Acter’s portfolio, accounting for 66% of total revenue in 1H26, vs. 69% in 2025. 
Foundry manufacturing is the largest source of demand for cleanrooms in Taiwan, 
especially as advanced-node capacity expands at sites in Hsinchu, Taichung and 
Kaohsiung. In the memory segment, the company executes turnkey engineering builds to 
expand its capacity aggressively, creating cleanroom opportunities across Taiwan, Japan 
and Singapore. This multi-sector coverage decreases the risk of cyclical downturns in any 
individual segment, in our view.

By establishing 33 operating entities across 11 international regions, the company 
effectively captures capex migration driven by global N+1 supply chain diversification. This 
allows Acter to support Taiwanese and international clients as they expand manufacturing 
capacity from Taiwan into Southeast Asia, Japan and the US. Due to the complexity of 
advanced-node IC manufacturing, packaging and testing, it is especially important for 
these Taiwanese semiconductor companies to work with familiar cleanroom builders when 
expanding in the US. This is one of the reasons why Taiwanese cleanroom companies are 
receiving overseas orders and starting to set up global bases, in our view. Despite higher 
management, material, and manpower costs in the US, most cleanroom companies are 
benefitting from the trend by improving margins, given the higher complexity and risks to 
operate overseas. Across overseas hubs in Texas and Phoenix (expected to begin from 
2027), Acter prioritises local hiring and partners with regional civil contractors to shorten 
the learning curve and to avoid the high overhead. To further address the potential labour 
shortages in the future, the company has established cross-border academic and 
vocational partnerships to bring Southeast Asian talent to Taiwan for technical training 
before deploying them back to their home markets, securing future human capacity to 
support global expansion projects.

By product, cleanroom electromechanical services remain Acter’s largest source of 
revenue (accounting for 66% of 1H26 revenue, per the company). These services include 
cleanroom architectural and MEP engineering, which powers airflow, filtration, 
environmental regulation and utility controls. Water, gas and chemical delivery systems are 
its second-largest source of revenue (accounting for 24% of 1H26 revenue, per the 
company). Contributed mainly by Novatech (6613 TT, not rated) and Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated), these services cover the main systems and hook-up systems, for  ultra-pure water 
(UPW), specialty gases, chemical piping, etc., all of which require highly precise build-up 
work. Except for cleanroom electromechanical and water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems, the rest of Acter’s revenue comes from the consumer industry services 
electromechanical (5%), biomedical (3%), and high-tech equipment and materials sales 
and services (1%).

Acter: revenue by product

Source: Company, Daiwa

Technically, Acter integrates digital transformation and green engineering into its business 
model, helping it differentiate from peers. Acter deploys digital twin technology and 1:1 3D 
BIM modelling to detect piping interferences and optimise spatial layout before installation, 
minimising schedule delays. This makes Acter a more reliable option, in our view, and thus 
being a favourable supplier, especially for the semiconductor clients that have extremely 
high quality and swiftness criterion.

Bright outlook: strong order visibility 
Rising orders on hand due to supply-demand imbalance for cleanrooms

TWD66bn in July 2026. We expect this figure to further increase in the following quarters, 
given the supply-demand imbalance in the cleanroom sector. This is composed of 
semiconductor (63%), electronic assembly (11%), consumer (9%) and others (17%), per 
the company. In 2H25, the company accelerated the digestion of small-scale projects 
below TWD1bn, and, as a result, starting from 1H26, it successfully shifted its project

(MEP) integration, especially for those data centres that implement liquid-cooling systems. 
The company also wins factory construction projects for Taiwanese ODMs across the US 
and Southeast Asia, as well as PCB facility projects in Thailand and China. Non-tech 
sectors, including biotechnology, commercial buildings, smart logistics hubs and medical 
facilities, comprise 9% of the total backlog, per management.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Competitive advantages 
High entry barriers to the segment 
Comprehensive product portfolio, empowered by subsidiaries 
Acter provides a one-stop EPCM solution, leveraging its subsidiaries to deliver complete 
solutions. Within the group, the parent company Acter focuses on overall cleanroom and 
MEP system integration; subsidiary Novatech provides primary chemical systems and 
hook-up systems; and sub-subsidiary Rayzer specialises in primary specialty-gas systems 
and hook-up systems. In China, Suzhou Winmax, also a subsidiary of Novatech, focuses 
on the supply of wet processing and chemical handling equipment. This integrated model 
covers cleanroom engineering integration, water, power, chemical and gas-utility systems, 
as well as post-completion validation and maintenance, ensuring cross-system 
compatibility while mitigating risks associated with multi-contractor hiring.

Under global supply chain diversification trends, this unified group strategy strengthens 
order-winning capabilities in overseas markets. Clients expanding internationally face 
regulatory, linguistic and supply-chain complexities, favouring partners that offer one-stop 
services with a proven cross-border track record.

A diverse client base helps Acter weather economic cycles 
Acter’s main competitive advantage is its diversification of clients by industry and region. 
This guides the company through the peaks and troughs of the economy, which is 
especially crucial given the strong cyclicality of the semiconductor/high-tech industry.  Acter 
serves a broad client base across semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, electronics assembly 
(ODM), CSP data centres, biopharmaceuticals and even the consumer industry (including 
commercial real estate and logistics), whereas many peers rely on a few large foundry 
clients. When semiconductor demand slows, capacity expansion in the PCB or data centre 
sectors offset the decline. Additionally, Acter avoids concentration risk and margin 
compression from major foundry accounts, as projects from leading foundries yield lower 
margins; by serving niche markets like US-based CSPs, the company achieves higher 
gross margins.

Geographically, Acter operates across Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia, the US and other 
countries. The company has OSAT and foundry customers in Taiwan and China, PCB 
clients expanding into Thailand, and ODM and data centre clients establishing operations 
in the US. Across different countries, local hiring and local supply chain implementation 
can further reduce costs and increase efficiency relative to peers reliant on a cross-border 
labour force.

Labour shortages 
Amid global labour shortages, Acter minimises the operational impact and turns workforce 
constraints into a moat through localised talent development. Since engineering labour 
cannot easily be automated, Acter has built a cross-border talent pipeline via industry-
academia partnerships. The company recruits Southeast Asian students for specialised 
M&E engineering training in Taiwan, deploying them to global project sites after their 
graduation to solve labour bottlenecks. This structure maintains an engineering team with 
an average tenure of more than 7 years, creating an operational barrier in an industry with 
a high employee turnover rate.

Compared with peers relying on high-cost personnel sent from Taiwan, we note that Acter’s 
local hiring strategy creates cost and efficiency advantages. Prioritising compliant local 
hiring across overseas markets reduces travel expenses while accelerating integration with 
local supply chains and regulations. Localised teams also handle post-completion services 
and technical support directly on-site, building long-term client trust in overseas markets.

### Page 11

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Numerous competitors, each with different specialisation 
Acter faces different competitors across its main product lines. In the EPCM segment, 
including cleanroom and MEP works, key competitors include UIS (2404 TT, not rated), 
L&K (6139 TT, not rated), Yankey (6691 TT, not rated) and Jiuhan (6903 TT, not rated). 
Despite having business models similar to Acter, UIS, Yankey and Jiuhan focus more on 
major semiconductor players in both Taiwan and the US, and L&K concentrates on ASEAN 
semiconductor projects. In the equipment/material sales and agency space, Acter mainly 
competes with Marketech (6196 TT, not rated). In the water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems area, through Novatech and Rayzher, Acter group competes with Marketech, 
Mega Union (6944 TT, not rated), Taiwan Puritic (6826 TT, not rated), Trusval (6667 TT, not 
rated) and Cica-Huntek (6725 TT, not rated).

In our view, despite the competitive environment, Acter maintains a formidable competitive 
moat built on several key advantages. We attribute this to its more than 45 year experience 
in the industry, enabling it to develop specialised expertise that is hard for competitors to 
replicate.

Financial analysis 
We forecast the company’s net profit to rise at a CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E, driven by 
a revenue CAGR of 24.6% due to surging demand from the high-tech industry (an increase 
in the order backlog to TWD66bn at the end of July 2026 vs. TWD46bn at end-2025) and a 
3.3pp expansion in operating margin over 2026-28E, driven by the gross margin 
improvement from more favourable project wins and operating leverage.

Strong order visibility, with revenue expected to grow 
across all sectors over 2026-28E 
We forecast Acter’s revenue to increase at a CAGR of 24.6% over 2026-28E, driven by 
scale expansion and a growing demand from various industries, including semiconductor, 
electronic assembly, optoelectronics and others.

Acter: revenue growth trend

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Gross- and operating-margin expansion trend  
Over 2026-28E, we forecast Acter’s gross and operating margins to expand on rising 
revenue scale and a higher complexity of cleanrooms for advanced manufacturing. We 
note that the gross margin from high-tech cleanrooms is higher than that from consumer 
industries, such as commercial buildings and biopharmaceuticals. Thus, we estimate the 
company’s gross margin to reach 26.0% in 2028E from 18.9% in 2025 and its operating 
margin to reach 21.6% in 2028E from 13.7% in 2025.

Acter: gross- and operating-margin trend

(from TWD6,325m in 2026E). We expect its net margin to come in at 13.1% in 2027E and 
14.2% in 2028E (vs. 11.7% in 2026E) and for its net profit to grow at a CAGR of 36.9% 
over 2026-28E.

Intact demand to support revenue growth over our 
forecast horizon; ROE expansion underway 
We forecast Acter’s total cash and equivalents to reach TWD16,838m (36.8% of its total 
assets) in 2026E and increase to TWD27,839m in 2028E (42.8% of total assets) with net 
cash likely to come in at TWD16,244m in 2026E and TWD27,245m in 2028E.

free cash flow (FCF) to improve to TWD12,129m in 2028E, from TWD5,763m in 2026E. 
We believe the capacity expansion cost will initially be low and that the company will 
continue to benefit from the better profitability.

We expect Acter’s ROE to improve to a record-high of 56.8% in 2028E from 29.3% in 2025 
(following steady annual increases to 46.8% in 2026E and 54.9% in 2027E), which we 
attribute to margin expansion from the revenue size increase and gross margin expansion 
led by increases in cleanroom complexity.

Valuation and risks  
Undemanding valuation, alongside its leading global 
positions in the OSAT and memory segments and an 
optimistic industry outlook  
We initiate coverage of Acter with a Buy (1) rating and 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based 
on a target PER of 20x applied to our one-year-forward EPS forecast. This target multiple 
is higher than the average of its past-3-year 1-year-forward PER of 12.2x (ranged from 
7.4x to 23.5x) and the average of its past-5-year 1-year-forward PER of 9.6x (ranged from 
4.4x to 23.5x), which we view as undemanding as we forecast a 36.9% net profit CAGR 
over 2026-28E, implying a 0.54x PEG. This is supported by its business expansion in the 
next 2-3 years. We see potential for a rerating of the stock, given Acter’s global leadership 
in cleanroom EPCM, particularly in the OSAT and memory industries.

The stock is trading currently at a 15.8x 1-year-forward PER vs. its earnings CAGR of 
36.9% over 2026-28E, translating to a 0.43x PEG ratio. Its share price has underperformed 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly due to its weaker-than-expected 2Q26 results. 
However, given the potential improvement in sector sentiment following TSMC’s results on 
15 October 2026, along with the likelihood that Acter’s revenue momentum will strengthen 
in the coming months, we recommend that investors buy the stock.

We compare Acter with other leading cleanroom EPCM companies such as UIS (2404 TT, 
TWD1,330.0, not rated), L&K (6139 TT, TWD787.0, not rated) and Yankey (6691 TT, 
TWD776.0, not rated). UIS, L&K and Yankey are trading at a PER of 14.7x (earnings 
CAGR of 40.7% over 2025-27E), 9.5x (earnings CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E) and 
15.1x (earnings CAGR of 53.6% over 2025-27E), on their 1-year-forward EPS, 
respectively.

Acter’s peers are trading at 18.7x for 2026E and 14.2x for 2027E. We forecast Acter to 
deliver an EPS CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E vs. 31.5% CAGR for the segment over the 
same period.

We believe our target one-year-forward PER of 20x (equivalent to a 0.54x PEG using 
2026-28E EPS CAGR) is undemanding as: 1) Acter is ramping up capacity to meet 
growing demand from high-tech customers and is in an expansion cycle; and 2) its 
leading position among OSAT and memory clients warrants a valuation premium, 
compared to its global peers.

Risks to our call  
Primary/key risks: 
Weaker-than-expected expansion in semiconductor industry 
The biggest downside risk to our call for Acter is weaker-than-expected expansion from 
semiconductor clients, especially in advanced-node foundries, advanced packaging and 
OSAT. If global demand for AI servers, HPC or HBM slows, or if clients delay fab build-ups 
and overseas expansion plans, it will directly pressure order inflows and backlog 
conversion for Acter.

Project execution delays or cancellations 
Acter’s revenue recognition is tied to large, complex EPCM and integrated engineering 
projects, which are often subject to customer-driven delays. Any postponement in project 
Our 12-month TP of 
TWD1,415 is also 
supported by our DCF 
valuation

### Page 18

18

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
schedule, construction bottlenecks or cancellation of large projects could defer revenue 
recognition and hurt overall profit. This risk is more obvious in overseas projects, where 
licensing, fire-safety approvals and hazardous-chemical regulations are more complicated.

Higher-than-expected raw-material price hikes 
Raw-material costs (especially copper and oil-related costs) account for 50-60% of COGS 
and thus the price volatility makes costs difficult to manage. Acter needs to maintain 
supplier relations, negotiate appropriate price-adjustment mechanisms with clients, and 
invest in R&D to identify composite material substitutes, thereby reducing reliance on any 
single component.

Secondary risks:  
Labour issues or larger-than-expected increases in labour costs  
Cleanroom engineering is highly labour-intensive and relies on on-site installation and 
supervision. Although Acter has built a local hiring and talent-development model, a larger-
than-expected shortage of engineers or rising wage pressure could still raise costs and 
constrain capacity expansion. Any inability to secure enough skilled labour may also limit 
the company’s ability to convert orders into revenue in time.

Margin pressure from lower-margin projects or mix shift 
Acter’s business mix includes a few low-margin C&S services as well as larger EPCM and 
system integration contracts. If the company takes on a higher proportion of lower-margin 
projects, or if competition intensifies in certain geographies or customer segments, gross 
margin could come under pressure. In addition, an unfavourable product mix shift towards 
less profitable work could offset benefits from backlog growth.

Overseas expansion and regulatory risks 
Acter is expanding across markets in the US, Southeast Asia, Japan and China, which 
exposes it to FX movements, local compliance requirements, tax complexity and regulatory 
uncertainty. Delays in licensing, changes in safety standards or local-content requirements 
could increase project costs and reduce execution efficiency. Any misstep in managing 
overseas operations could damage profitability and client retention. 
Key risks to our call: 
weaker-than-expected 
expansion in

project delays or 
cancellations, and 
higher-than-expected

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Appendix 
Company background 
History 
Acter (5536 TT), founded in 1979 and headquartered in Taichung, Taiwan, is a cleanroom 
EPCM provider with over four decades of experience. Starting with early mechanical and 
electrical (M&E) engineering through vertical and horizontal integration, Acter has now 
developed into an engineering technology group with cross-border execution capabilities. A 
cleanroom building process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the 
physical plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for 
essential utility generation, where Acter is one of the experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
networks, and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. The 
company’s core competency is providing one-stop integrated total solutions, spanning 
services, primary piping networks and hook-up systems, leaving C&S services to other 
engineering peers, given the nature of the latter segment’s low margin and long timespan.

In terms of business model, Acter delivers EPCM (Engineering, Procurement, 
Construction, Management) capabilities. From initial customised planning and feasibility 
assessments to strategic procurement, installation, and post-completion maintenance, 
Acter builds production environments tailored to client process requirements.

Acter: EPCM solutions offered

Source: Company, Daiwa

Regarding global expansion, Acter actively responds to the global supply chain’s “N+1” 
strategy, operating across 11 regions with 33 companies globally. Its service network spans 
Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia (including Thailand, Malaysia, Vietnam, Indonesia, and 
Singapore), Japan, India, and the US. The company leverages a group synergy strategy 
by integrating resources from key subsidiaries – such as Novatech (6613 TT, not rated; 
provides chemical primary and hook-up systems), Rayzher (7703 TT, not rated; provides 
special gas primary and hook-up supply systems), and Acter Group (603163 CH, not rated; 
provides cleanroom engineering in China) – to form a complete technical ecosystem, 
ensuring cross-border capability and quality.

Acter: global footprint

Source: Company, Daiwa

In 2Q26, Acter classified revenue as: cleanroom electromechanical (63%), water, gas and 
chemical delivery systems (28%), consumer industry electromechanical (6%), biomedical 
(2%) and high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (1%). In 2025, the product mix 
was: cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas and chemical delivery systems 
(20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and high-tech 
equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

Acter serves a diversified client base divided into high-tech and consumer divisions. The 
high-tech division focuses on advanced sectors including semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, AI 
data centres (for CSPs), and electronics assembly. The consumer division extends into 
healthcare, premium commercial properties, logistics facilities, and green engineering. This 
multi-industry strategy allows the company to capitalise on tech sector upgrades while 
maintaining resilient operations across technology (especially semiconductor) cycles.

Acter adopts a management strategy involving local hiring in overseas markets, prioritising 
collaboration with local supply chains and compliance with local regulations to reduce 
cross-border deployment costs and thus build strong local teams. Acter has more than 
2,600 employees worldwide (average age: 42 years; average length of service: 7 years). 
With more than 45 years of experience in EPCM, the company plays a reliable role for both 
high-tech and consumer industry.

recycling, and AI smart facility management systems to help clients achieve carbon 
neutrality while expanding capacity. Backed by solid corporate governance, Acter

Glossary of terms 
EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management): Cleanroom 
construction services that cover the whole process including project design, purchasing, 
construction and management.

BIM (building information modelling): A digital modelling system of a building and its key 
information used in cleanroom construction.

C&S (civil & structural): Site infrastructure and the design and construction of load-
bearing elements.

MEP (mechanical, electrical, and plumbing): Building essential systems for environment 
control, power, lighting, water, and drainage.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 7 Oct 2026 
Source:  Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Acter maintains a structured governance framework that supports disciplined management. The
chairman is Chin-Li Liang. The company operates with defined responsibility across the board,
management, cyber security oversight, operations, and audit functions. It has established an
organisation function led by the President, a Chief Cyber Security Officer, a Response Team, an
Operations and Policy Enforcement Team, and an Audit Team to supervise policy execution and
review security implementation.
Capital management 1
Acter manages capital in a stable and shareholder-friendly manner. Over the past several years,
Acter has typically maintained a dividend payout ratio of 65-80%. It also maintains a strong
financing network across multiple banks, supporting flexibility for project execution and
expansion while preserving financial stability.
Related party &
transaction 1
Acter’s business is supported by a transparent network of suppliers, project partners, and
financial counterparties, including companies with equity ties and reciprocal business
relationships that are common in its operating ecosystem. These relationships are managed
through formal internal procedures for fund lending, endorsements and guarantees, and asset
acquisition and disposal, all of which are approved through shareholder mechanisms and
company rules. Such a structure helps keep inter-company and supply-chain transactions
organised and traceable, reducing the risk of unclear dealings.
E Product quality &safety 1
Acter executes its projects through a complete, disciplined process covering bidding
assessment, project planning, design, procurement, construction management, verification and
testing, completion and acceptance, closing review, and warranty service. Its cleanroom and
electromechanical engineering business is built around maintaining temperature, humidity, and
dust-free environments that support high-precision manufacturing. The company strengthens
quality control through BIM, Revit, simulation software, ISO drawing output tools, and automated
modeling, which improve design accuracy, construction efficiency, and project consistency.
E Ecological impacts 1
Acter has built a concrete sustainability programme with measurable environmental outcomes.
The company completed Scope 1, 2, and 3 greenhouse gas emissions calculation, obtained
third-party verification under ISO 14064-1:2018, and publicly disclosed energy consumption and
emissions data. It also implemented a strong waste reduction programme, achieving a 95%
wastepaper recycling rate at headquarters in 2025 and 100% paper recycling at the 19F and 20F
floors after establishing paper segregation and reuse procedures. The company also promoted
sustainability measures through emissions inventory management, environmental disclosures,
and resource-efficiency practices. These actions show active environmental management and a
clear commitment to lowering operational impact.
Health & safety

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION / EUROZONE
Strength on multiple fronts. Business activity in September, mea -
sured by the composite PMI, reached its highest level since April 2023
(53.1, +1.1pt). This was led by a strong improvement in the services
index (53.0, +1.4 pt) while the manufacturing PMI was stable and

comfortably in expansion (52.7). Input and output price indices both
rose sharply, especially services output prices (56.1, highest since Fe -
bruary 2024) indicating the energy price shock is spreading. New car
registrations in the EU rose 4.5% y/y in August and 5.3% year-to-date
in August. Electric cars’ market share continues to rise (to 27.7% of
new registrations year to date). Less positively, bank loans to corpo -
rates halved in August compared to July. On the trade front, the EU

has adopted rare provisional safeguard measures (quotas and tariffs)
against a type of industrial steel products, in response to a recent
surge in imports from China. It also reached a “substantial agree -
ment” on a new trade deal with The Philippines. As part of its efforts
to safeguard the role of the euro in DLT-finance, the ECB has launched
its new 'Pontes' project, which is a first step toward providing a digi -
tal euro for wholesale uses. Pontes creates a link between the TAR -

GET2 interbank settlement system in central bank money and private
DLTs. The ECB also announced it intends to purchase a limited amount
of tokenised government bonds through its own balance sheet, to
gain hands-on expertise in the digital asset ecosystem.
Upcoming: September European Commission Economic Sentiment In -
dex (Tuesday).
ECB leadership race kick-started. While ECB watchers have been on
tenterhooks for an official announcement by President Lagarde of an

early departure (her term ends in October 2027), the news came from
ECB Board member Isabel Schnabel announcing she would leave her
role early, on January 3rd, 2027, to take on the role of Financial Coun -
sellor at the IMF. The Eurogroup of finance ministers is now expected
to take a package approach to choosing successors for the President,
as the Board seat left vacant by Ms. Schnabel and the Chief Econo -
mist post is due to become vacant in May. The final decision on the

Presidency rests with European Leaders. Country and gender balance
considerations will be paramount.
France. Back to expansion, but mixed signals. The flash composite
PMI returned to expansion for the first time since November 2025,
reaching 51.2 in September after 48.5 in August. The improvement is
driven by services, at 51.4 (+3.4 m/m), while the manufacturing PMI
fell slightly to 50.3 (from 51.1 in August). Insee's business climate,
however, fell back to 96 in September from 98 in August (100 = long-

term average). It was weighed down by services, retail and wholesale
trade. The business climate was stable in the manufacturing sector
(101) and construction (96). Both surveys concur on the fact that
selling price pressures are increasing in the manufacturing sector.
Consumer confidence was stable for the 3 rd consecutive month, at 86
in September (20-year average: 93). The government has announced
an increase in fuel subsidies amounting to EUR 450 million. This mea-

sure is not expected to deteriorate further the fiscal deficit (projected
by the government at 5.4% of GDP in 2026).
Upcoming: September Inflation (Wednesday); Draft budget law and
final manufacturing PMI (Thursday)
Germany. Business activity and sentiment improve further. The com -
posite PMI for September was above expectations by rising to 53.8
(+2 pts m/m), its highest level in 11 months. This was driven by the
services sector returning to growth after five months of contraction

(52.9; +3.2 pts m/m). Growth in the manufacturing sector leveled
off slightly but remains robust. The production index stood at 55.9
(-0.8 pt m/m). Companies are reporting further signs of resilience,
with an acceleration in production, stable outlooks, rising employ -
ment in services, and stabilisation in manufacturing. Price pressures
are elevated and, in most categories, rising. Meanwhile, the Sep -
tember Ifo index of business climate rose to its highest since May

2023 (89.9; +1.2pts). Expectations for business development in the
next 6 months rose to an 11-month high of 90.4 (+1.4pts). All sectors
saw improvements in their indicators. Consumer confidence deterio -
rated further, however: the GfK index deteriorated to -30.6 in October
(-3.8 pts m/m, its lowest since last May), driven by a significant drop
in income expectations. Still, the decline in consumer sentiment was
partially mitigated by a fifth month of improving economic expecta -

tions.
Upcoming: August retail sales, September unemployment rate and
flash inflation (Wednesday).
UNITED KINGDOM
Rate hike increasingly likely, as indicated by the Bank of England’s
Governor and two Deputy Governors (owing to the persistence of the
energy price shock). Resilient activity points in the same direction:
September PMIs showed continued expansion (composite fell slightly
to 51.7), with services slowing down (51.7; -0.8 m/m), and manufac -

turing accelerating (to 52, +0.3 m/m) due to resilient demand in AI
and defence. Business optimism held steady at a six-month high and
manufacturing confidence reached its strongest point since Februa -
ry on robust investment and new projects, offsetting a slight dip in
services caused by geopolitical concerns. GfK Consumer Climate unex-
pectedly rose to -13 in September (+1 m/m), hitting its highest level
since August 2024 due to improved personal finance and economic

outlooks.
Upcoming: Q2 GDP Final (Thursday); BoE DMP Firms Price Expecta -
tions survey (Friday).
UNITED STATES
A ‘good news is bad news’ week. PMIs are typically little watched in
the US owing to low correlation to GDP , nevertheless this week they
were a key trigger for a brutal selloff in US yields across the curve,
featuring 20+ basis point moves and a very weak 5Y bond auction.
Hawkish Fed speakers talk also contributed (Barr, Williams, Paulson).

The preliminary September composite PMI reading surged to 58.4, a
five-year high, defying expectations of a drop. Both the Services and
Manufacturing PMIs rose to multi-year highs. New orders, employ -
ment, and input prices all rose sharply, with input price growth ac -
celerating. Core capital goods orders (non-defence, ex-aircraft) rose
1.6% m/m in August, well above expectations, after an upwardly re -
vised +0.6% m/m in July — extending the momentum in business

equipment investment. The US 30-year yield rose to 5.53%, its highest
since 2004. The 10Y touched 5.23%. 30-year mortgage rates are at 7%.
Upcoming: GDP final estimate for Q2, August PCE report (Wednes -
day); September ISM manufacturing (Thursday); September non-
farm payroll/employment report (Friday).
